\displaystyle\lesssim|\varkappa|^{-(2s+1)}\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\left(\|q\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}\right),

(3.35)

\displaystyle|\varkappa|\|\gamma\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|\gamma\|_{X^{\sigma+1}_{\kappa}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\varkappa|^{-(s+\frac{1}{2})}\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{\varkappa}}\left(\|q\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}\right),

where the implicit constants are independent of
\kappa,\varkappa
.

Proof.

Applying the product estimate (
2.15
) with the quadratic identity (
3.31
) and the symmetry relation (
3.15
), we may bound

\displaystyle|\varkappa|\|\gamma\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|\gamma\|_{X^{\sigma+1}_{\kappa}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\varkappa|^{-(s+\frac{1}{2})}\|\gamma\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s+1}_{\varkappa}}\left(|\varkappa|\|\gamma\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|\gamma\|_{X^{\sigma+1}_{\kappa}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+|\varkappa|^{-(s+\frac{1}{2})}\max_{\pm\varkappa}\|g_{12}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s+1}_{\varkappa}}\max_{\pm\varkappa}\left(|\varkappa|\|g_{12}\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|g_{12}\|_{X^{\sigma+1}_{\kappa}}\right).

In view of (
3.27
), taking
0<\delta\ll 1
sufficiently small (independently of
\varkappa
) and using (
3.24
), we get

(3.36)

|\varkappa|\|\gamma\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|\gamma\|_{X^{\sigma+1}_{\kappa}}\lesssim|\varkappa|^{-(s+\frac{1}{2})}\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{\varkappa}}\max_{\pm\varkappa}\left(|\varkappa|\|g_{12}\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|g_{12}\|_{X^{\sigma+1}_{\kappa}}\right).

As a consequence, the estimate (
3.35
) follows from the estimate (
3.33
).

To prove the estimate (
3.33
) we first apply the estimate (
2.14
) to obtain

(3.37)

|\varkappa|\|g_{12}\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|g_{12}\|_{X^{\sigma+1}_{\kappa}}\lesssim\|q\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}+|\varkappa|\|g_{12}^{[\geq 3]}\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|g_{12}^{[\geq 3]}\|_{X^{\sigma+1}_{\kappa}}.

From the identity (
3.12
) for
g_{12}
, we see that
g_{12}^{[\geq 3]}=-(2\varkappa-\partial)^{-1}(q\gamma)
. Thus, employing (
2.14
), we find

|\varkappa|\|g_{12}^{[\geq 3]}\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|g_{12}^{[\geq 3]}\|_{X^{\sigma+1}_{\kappa}}\lesssim\|q\gamma\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|q\gamma\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}.

To continue, we use (
2.16
) together with (
3.27
) and (
3.36
) for
\gamma
to obtain

\|q\gamma\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}\lesssim|\varkappa|^{-(2s+1)}\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\Bigl[\|q\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\max_{\pm\varkappa}\bigl(|\varkappa|\|g_{12}\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|g_{12}\|_{X^{\sigma+1}_{\kappa}}\bigr)\Bigr].

Using (
2.1
) and (
3.27
), we may bound

\displaystyle\|q\gamma\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\varkappa|^{-(2s+1)}\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{\varkappa}}^{2}.

As a consequence,

\displaystyle\max_{\pm\varkappa}\bigl(|\varkappa|\|g_{12}^{[\geq 3]}\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|g_{12}^{[\geq 3]}\|_{X^{\sigma+1}_{\kappa}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim|\varkappa|^{-(2s+1)}\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\Bigl[\|q\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}+\max_{\pm\varkappa}\bigl(|\varkappa|\|g_{12}\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|g_{12}\|_{X^{\sigma+1}_{\kappa}}\bigr)\Bigr].

Combining this with (
3.37
) and choosing
0<\delta\ll 1
sufficiently small (independently of
\kappa,\varkappa
), we obtain (
3.33
) and so also (
3.34
).
∎

Due to the structure of our microscopic conservation law, the functions
g_{12}
and
\gamma
will frequently occur in the combination
\frac{g_{12}(\varkappa)}{2+\gamma(\varkappa)}
. Naturally, this too may be written as a power series in
q
and
r
and we adapt our square brackets notation accordingly:

\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}=\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[1]}+\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[\geq 3]}=\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[1]}+\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[3]}+\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[\geq 5]},

where the leading order terms are given by

(3.38)

\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[1]}=\tfrac{1}{2}g_{12}^{[1]}\quad\text{and}\quad\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[3]}=\tfrac{1}{2}g_{12}^{[3]}-\tfrac{1}{4}g_{12}^{[1]}\gamma^{[2]},

and the remainders by

(3.39)

\displaystyle\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[\geq 3]}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}g_{12}^{[\geq 3]}-\tfrac{g_{12}\gamma}{2(2+\gamma)},

(3.40)

\displaystyle\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[\geq 5]}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}g_{12}^{[\geq 5]}-\tfrac{1}{4}g_{12}^{[1]}\gamma^{[\geq 4]}-\tfrac{1}{4}g_{12}^{[\geq 3]}\gamma+\tfrac{g_{12}\gamma^{2}}{4(2+\gamma)}.

Our earlier results yield the following information about these quantities:

Corollary 3.5
.

Let
\sigma\in\{s+\frac{1}{2},s+1\}
. Then there exists
\delta>0
so that for all real
|\kappa|\geq 1
and
|\varkappa|\geq 1
, we have the estimates

(3.41)

\displaystyle|\varkappa|\bigl\|\tfrac{g_{12}(\varkappa)}{2+\gamma(\varkappa)}\bigr\|_{H^{s}}+\bigl\|\tfrac{g_{12}(\varkappa)}{2+\gamma(\varkappa)}\bigr\|_{H^{s+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|q\|_{H^{s}},

(3.42)

\displaystyle|\varkappa|\bigl\|\big(\tfrac{g_{12}(\varkappa)}{2+\gamma(\varkappa)}\big)^{[\geq 3]}\bigr\|_{H^{s}}+\bigl\|\big(\tfrac{g_{12}(\varkappa)}{2+\gamma(\varkappa)}\big)^{[\geq 3]}\bigr\|_{H^{s+1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\varkappa|^{-(2s+1)}\|q\|_{H^{s}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\|q\|_{H^{s}},

for any
q\in B_{\delta}
. Moreover, for
q\in\mathcal{C}([-1,1];B_{\delta})\cap X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}
,

(3.43)

\displaystyle|\varkappa|\bigl\|\tfrac{g_{12}(\varkappa)}{2+\gamma(\varkappa)}\bigr\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\bigl\|\tfrac{g_{12}(\varkappa)}{2+\gamma(\varkappa)}\bigr\|_{X^{\sigma+1}_{\kappa}}\lesssim\|q\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}},

(3.44)

\displaystyle|\varkappa|\bigl\|\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[\geq 3]}\bigr\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\bigl\|\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[\geq 3]}\bigr\|_{X^{\sigma+1}_{\kappa}}\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim|\varkappa|^{-(2s+1)}\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\Big(\|q\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}\Big),

(3.45)

\displaystyle|\varkappa|\bigl\|\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[\geq 5]}\bigr\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\bigl\|\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[\geq 5]}\bigr\|_{X^{\sigma+1}_{\kappa}}\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim|\varkappa|^{-2(2s+1)}\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{\varkappa}}^{4}\Big(\|q\|_{X^{\sigma}_{\kappa}}+\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}\Big),

where
g_{12}=g_{12}(\varkappa)
and
\gamma=\gamma(\varkappa)
.

Proof.

From (
3.38
) and (
3.20
), we see that

|\varkappa|\bigl\|\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[1]}\bigr\|_{H^{s}}+\bigl\|\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[1]}\bigr\|_{H^{s+1}}\approx\bigl\|(2\varkappa-\partial)\big(\tfrac{g_{12}(\varkappa)}{2+\gamma(\varkappa)}\big)^{[1]}\bigr\|_{H^{s}}\approx\|q\|_{H^{s}}.

Thus (
3.41
) will follow once we prove (
3.42
). Moreover, using also (
3.12
), we find

(2\varkappa-\partial)\big(\tfrac{g_{12}}{2+\gamma}\big)^{[\geq 3]}=-\tfrac{\gamma}{2(2+\gamma)}q+\tfrac{g_{12}}{(2+\gamma)^{2}}\gamma^{\prime}

and thence

LHS(
3.42
)

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigl\|\tfrac{\gamma}{2+\gamma}q\bigr\|_{H^{s}}+\bigl\|\tfrac{g_{12}}{(2+\gamma)^{2}}\gamma^{\prime}\bigr\|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\varkappa|^{-(s+\frac{1}{2})}\|q\|_{H^{s}}\bigl\|\tfrac{\gamma}{2+\gamma}\bigr\|_{H^{s+1}_{\varkappa}}+|\varkappa|^{-(2s+1)}\|q\|_{H^{s}}\|q\|_{H^{s}_{\varkappa}}\bigl\|\tfrac{g_{12}}{(2+\gamma)^{2}}\bigr\|_{H^{s+1}_{\varkappa}},

where the second step was an application of (
2.1
) and (
3.27
). To handle the remaining rational functions, we expand as series and employ the algebra property (
2.5
), together with (
3.24
) and (
3.27
). This yields (
3.41
) for
\delta>0
sufficiently small.

Next we prove (
3.44
), since (
3.43
) follows from this, (
3.38
), and (
2.14
).

In order to prove (
3.44
), we first employ (
3.39
). The requisite estimate for the first term was given already in Lemma
3.4
. The second summand can be treated by combining that lemma with the algebra property (
2.17
).

It remains to prove (
3.45
). Recalling the expansion (
3.40
) the last two terms are easily controlled using (
3.34
), (
3.35
), (
3.43
), and Lemma
2.4
. To control the first two terms we use (
3.12
) and (
3.32
).
∎

4.
Conservation laws and dynamics

At a formal level, the logarithmic perturbation determinant
\log\det(L_{0}^{-1}L)
(multiplied by
\sgn(\kappa)
) is given by

\sgn(\kappa)\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{\ell-1}}{\ell}\tr\left\{\left(\sqrt{R_{0}}\left(L-L_{0}\right)\sqrt{R_{0}}\right)^{\ell}\right\}.

For
\ell>1
the trace is well defined because the operator is trace class. For
\ell=1
this fails; however, in view of (
3.6
) it is natural to regard the trace as being zero in this case. In fact, (
3.6
) implies that only the even
\ell
contribute to this sum.

With this in mind, we adopt the following as our rigorous definition of
A
:

(4.1)

A(\kappa;q):=\sgn(\kappa)\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\tfrac{(-1)^{m-1}}{m}\tr\left\{(\Lambda\Gamma)^{m}\right\}.

We will prove the convergence of this series in Lemma
4.1
below, as well as deriving several other basic properties.

The quantity
A
is readily seen to be closely related to the quantity
\alpha(\kappa;q)
that formed the center point of the analysis in
[
38
]
. Concretely, for
\kappa\geq 1
,

(4.2)

\displaystyle\alpha(\kappa;q)=\pm\operatorname{Re}A(\kappa;q)=\pm\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl[A(\kappa;q)-A(-\kappa;q)\bigr];

see (
4.3
) below. In that paper, it was shown that
\alpha(q)
is preserved under the NLS and mKdV flows. In fact, the argument given there even shows that
A(\kappa;q)
is conserved. However, for our purposes here, we need several stronger assertions of a similar flavor.

First, we need that
A(\kappa;q)
is conserved under all flows generated by the real and imaginary parts of
A(\varkappa;q)
for general
\varkappa
. This is proved in Lemma
4.3
below and will yield the conservation of
\alpha
under our regularized Hamiltonians. This allows us to obtain a priori bounds for these regularized flows.

Second, we rely on our discovery of a microscopic expression of the conservation of
A
; this will be essential in our development of local smoothing estimates. The relevant density
\rho
is introduced in Lemma
4.1
; see (
4.6
). The corresponding currents (for various flows) are collected in Corollary
4.14
, building on a number of intermediate results.

Lemma 4.1

(Properties of
A
)
.

There exists
\delta>0
so that for all
q\in B_{\delta}
and real
|\kappa|\geq 1
, the series (
4.1
) defining
A
converges absolutely. Moreover,

(4.3)

\displaystyle A(\kappa)=-\bar{A}(-\kappa),

(4.4)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta q}=g_{21},\qquad\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta r}=-g_{12},\qquad\gamma^{\prime}=2\left(q\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta q}-r\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta r}\right),

(4.5)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\partial A}{\partial\kappa}=\int\gamma(x;\kappa)\,dx\quad\text{and}\quad A(\kappa)=-\int_{\kappa}^{\sgn(\kappa)\infty}\int\gamma(x;\varkappa)\,dx\,d\varkappa,

(4.6)

\displaystyle A=\int\rho(x;\kappa)\,dx\quad\text{where}\quad\rho(\kappa)=\frac{qg_{21}(\kappa)-rg_{12}(\kappa)}{2+\gamma(\kappa)}.

Proof.

First, we observe that the series (
4.1
) converges absolutely and uniformly for
|\kappa|\geq 1
and
q\in B_{\delta}
, provided
0<\delta\ll 1
. This follows from the estimate (
3.7
). In the same way, convergence holds for the term-wise derivative of the series (
4.1
) with respect to
\kappa
. The terms appearing are exactly those from (
3.18
) and (
3.19
), and so we may deduce that

\frac{\partial A}{\partial\kappa}=\int\gamma(x;\kappa)\,dx.

This proves the first assertion in (
4.5
) as well as justifying
1.10
. The second assertion of (
4.5
) then follows since (
3.7
) guarantees that
A(\kappa)\to 0
uniformly on
B_{\delta}
as
|\kappa|\to\infty
.

The conjugation symmetry (
4.3
) follows immediately from (
4.5
) and (
3.15
).

Differentiating the series (
4.1
) with respect to
r
yields the series (
3.19
) for
g_{12}
with an additional minus sign, thus giving the second assertion in (
4.4
). The first assertion follows in a parallel manner, or by invoking conjugation symmetry. The third part of (
4.4
) follows from the first two parts via (
3.11
).

We now turn our attention to (
4.6
). First we must clarify what we mean by
\int\rho
. When
q\in\mathscr{S}
, then
\rho
also belongs to Schwartz class (for
\delta
small enough) and so the integral can be taken in the classical sense. For
q\in H^{s}
however, we interpret this integral via the duality between
H^{s}
and
H^{-s}
, noting that

q,r\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R})\quad\text{and}\quad\tfrac{g_{21}(\kappa)}{2+\gamma(\kappa)},\tfrac{g_{12}(\kappa)}{2+\gamma(\kappa)}\in H^{1+s}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow H^{-s}(\mathbb{R});

see Corollary
3.5
. By density and continuity, it suffices to verify (
4.6
) for
q\in\mathscr{S}
.

Differentiating (
3.12
), (
3.13
), and (
3.31
) with respect to
\kappa
and then combining these with the original versions shows

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\Bigl(g_{12}\tfrac{\partial g_{21}}{\partial\kappa}-\tfrac{\partial g_{12}}{\partial\kappa}g_{21}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=-\gamma(2+\gamma)+(1+\gamma)\tfrac{\partial\ }{\partial\kappa}\bigl(qg_{21}-rg_{12}\bigr)-\bigl(qg_{21}-rg_{12}\bigr)\tfrac{\partial\gamma}{\partial\kappa}.

Using also (
3.11
) we obtain

\displaystyle-\bigl(g_{12}\tfrac{\partial g_{21}}{\partial\kappa}-\tfrac{\partial g_{12}}{\partial\kappa}g_{21}\bigr)\gamma^{\prime}=-\gamma(2+\gamma)\tfrac{\partial\ }{\partial\kappa}\bigl(qg_{21}-rg_{12}\bigr)+\bigl(qg_{21}-rg_{12}\bigr)(1+\gamma)\tfrac{\partial\gamma}{\partial\kappa}.

These identities then combine to show

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\frac{g_{12}\tfrac{\partial g_{21}}{\partial\kappa}-\tfrac{\partial g_{12}}{\partial\kappa}g_{21}}{2+\gamma}

\displaystyle=-\gamma+\frac{\partial\ }{\partial\kappa}\frac{qg_{21}-rg_{12}}{2+\gamma},

which can then be integrated in
x
to yield

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\ }{\partial\kappa}\int\frac{qg_{21}-rg_{12}}{2+\gamma}\,dx=\int\gamma\,dx=\frac{\partial A}{\partial\kappa}.

The veracity of (
4.6
) then follows by observing that both sides of (
4.6
) vanish in the limit
|\kappa|\to\infty
.
∎

Next, we show that our basic Hamiltonians arise as coefficients in the asymptotic expansion of
A(\kappa)
as
\kappa\to\infty
. This will also be important for introducing our renormalized flows later on.

Lemma 4.2
.

For
q\in B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
,

(4.7)

A(\kappa)=\tfrac{1}{2\kappa}M+\tfrac{-i}{(2\kappa)^{2}}P+\tfrac{(-i)^{2}}{(2\kappa)^{3}}H_{\mathrm{NLS}}+\tfrac{(-i)^{3}}{(2\kappa)^{4}}H_{\mathrm{mKdV}}+O(\kappa^{-5})

as an asymptotic series on Schwartz class.

Proof.

While the first few terms can readily be discovered by brute force, we follow a systematic method based on the biHamiltonian relations

(4.8)

\displaystyle-2\kappa\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta q}

\displaystyle=\partial\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta q}-r\bigl[\gamma+1\bigr]=\partial\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta q}+2r\partial^{-1}\bigl(r\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta r}-q\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta q}\bigr)-r,

\displaystyle 2\kappa\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta r}

\displaystyle=\partial\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta r}+q\bigl[\gamma+1\bigr]=\partial\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta r}-2q\partial^{-1}\bigr(r\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta r}-q\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta q}\bigr)+q,

which, in view of (
4.4
), are merely a recapitulation of (
3.12
) and (
3.13
).

By iterating (
4.8
), we find

(4.9)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta r}

\displaystyle=-g_{12}=\tfrac{q}{2\kappa}+\tfrac{q^{\prime}}{(2\kappa)^{2}}+\tfrac{q^{\prime\prime}-2q^{2}r}{(2\kappa)^{3}}+\tfrac{q^{\prime\prime\prime}-6qq^{\prime}r}{(2\kappa)^{4}}+O(\kappa^{-5}),

\displaystyle\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta q}

\displaystyle=g_{21}=\tfrac{r}{2\kappa}-\tfrac{r^{\prime}}{(2\kappa)^{2}}+\tfrac{r^{\prime\prime}-2qr^{2}}{(2\kappa)^{3}}-\tfrac{r^{\prime\prime\prime}-6qrr^{\prime}}{(2\kappa)^{4}}+O(\kappa^{-5}),

which can then be integrated to recover the series for
A
; indeed,

A(q)=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\theta}A(\theta q)\,d\theta=\int_{0}^{1}\langle\bar{q},\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta q}(\theta q)\rangle+\langle\bar{r},\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta r}(\theta q)\rangle\,d\theta.

In following this algorithm, we have found it convenient to successively update the asymptotic expansion of
\gamma
using (
3.31
), rather than compute
\partial^{-1}(r\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta r}-q\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta q})
by laboriously finding complete derivatives. We record here the key result:

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\gamma=-\tfrac{qr}{(2\kappa)^{2}}-\tfrac{q^{\prime}r-qr^{\prime}}{(2\kappa)^{3}}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{q^{\prime\prime}r-q^{\prime}r^{\prime}+qr^{\prime\prime}-3q^{2}r^{2}}{(2\kappa)^{4}}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{q^{\prime\prime\prime}r-q^{\prime\prime}r^{\prime}+q^{\prime}r^{\prime\prime}-qr^{\prime\prime\prime}-6qq^{\prime}r^{2}+6q^{2}rr^{\prime}}{(2\kappa)^{5}}+O(\kappa^{-6}).

This technique is easily automated on a computer algebra system, which we have done as a check on our hand computations.
∎

Although the mechanical interpretation of the Poisson bracket (
1.1
) originates in real-valued observables
F
and
G
, the definition makes sense for complex-valued functions as well. In view of the conjugation symmetry (
4.3
), the following guarantees the commutation of both the real and imaginary parts of
A
:

Lemma 4.3

(Poisson brackets)
.

There exists
\delta>0
so that for all real
|\kappa|,|\varkappa|\geq 1
and
q\in B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
we have

(4.10)

\{A(\kappa),A(\varkappa)\}=0.

Proof.

If
\kappa=\varkappa
there is nothing to prove. Suppose now that
\kappa\neq\varkappa
. From (
4.4
) and then (
3.14
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\{A(\kappa),A(\varkappa)\}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{i}\int g_{12}(\kappa)g_{21}(\varkappa)-g_{21}(\kappa)g_{12}(\varkappa)=0.\qed

As shown already in
[
38
]
, the conservation of
A(\kappa)
leads to global in time control on the
H^{s}
norm. Rather than simply recapitulate that argument, which was based on the series (
4.1
), we will present a proof that brings the density
\rho
to center stage. This approach will be essential later, when we introduce localizations; see Lemmas
5.2
and
6.3
.

Proposition 4.4

(A priori bound)
.

There exists
\delta>0
so that for all
q\in B_{\delta}
and
\kappa\geq 1
we have

(4.11)

\int_{\kappa}^{\infty}\varkappa^{2s+1}\alpha(\varkappa)\,\frac{d\varkappa}{\varkappa}\approx_{s}\|q\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}.

Choosing
\delta>0
even smaller if necessary, we deduce the a priori estimate

(4.12)

\|q(t)\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}\quad\text{uniformly for}\quad q(0)\in B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}

for any Hamiltonian flow that is continuous on Schwartz class and preserves
A(\varkappa)
for all
|\varkappa|\geq 1
.

Proof.

We first decompose
\rho(\varkappa)=\rho^{[2]}(\varkappa)+\rho^{[\geq 4]}(\varkappa)
with

(4.13)

\displaystyle\rho^{[2]}(\varkappa)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(q\cdot\tfrac{r}{2\varkappa+\partial}+\tfrac{q}{2\varkappa-\partial}\cdot r\big),

(4.14)

\displaystyle\rho^{[\geq 4]}(\varkappa)

\displaystyle=q\cdot\big(\tfrac{g_{21}(\varkappa)}{2+\gamma(\varkappa)}\big)^{[\geq 3]}-r\cdot(\tfrac{g_{12}(\varkappa)}{2+\gamma(\varkappa)}\big)^{[\geq 3]}.

Inspired by (
4.2
), we compute

(4.15)

\displaystyle\pm\int\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl[\rho^{[2]}(x;\varkappa)-\rho^{[2]}(x;-\varkappa)\bigr]\,dx=2\varkappa\int\frac{|\hat{q}(\xi)|^{2}\,d\xi}{4\varkappa^{2}+\xi^{2}}

and so, invoking (
2.7
), deduce that

(4.16)

\displaystyle\pm\int_{\kappa}^{\infty}\!\int\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl[\rho^{[2]}(x;\varkappa)-\rho^{[2]}(x;-\varkappa)\bigr]\varkappa^{2s}\,dx\,d\varkappa\approx\|q\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}.

On the other hand, interpolating the bounds in (
3.42
), we find

(4.17)

\displaystyle\bigl\|\big(\tfrac{g_{21}(\varkappa)}{2+\gamma(\varkappa)}\big)^{[\geq 3]}\bigr\|_{H^{-s}}\lesssim|\varkappa|^{-2(1+2s)}\delta\|q\|_{H^{s}_{\varkappa}}^{2}

and consequently,

(4.18)

\displaystyle\int_{\kappa}^{\infty}\biggl|\int\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl[\rho^{[\geq 4]}(x;\varkappa)-\rho^{[\geq 4]}(x;-\varkappa)\bigr]\varkappa^{2s}\,dx\biggr|d\varkappa\lesssim\kappa^{-(1+2s)}\delta^{2}\|q\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}.

Thus (
4.11
) follows by choosing
\delta>0
sufficiently small.

To deduce (
4.12
), we exploit continuity in time.
∎

Proposition
4.4
is the key to proving equicontinuity of orbits. The proper extension of the notion of equicontinuity from the setting of the Arzelà–Ascoli Theorem to Sobolev spaces was discussed already by M. Riesz
[
51
]
.

Definition 4.5

(Equicontinuity)
.

A set
Q\subset H^{s}
is said to be
equicontinuous
if

\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\sup_{q\in Q}\sup_{|y|<\delta}\|q(\cdot+y)-q(\cdot)\|_{H^{s}}=0.

Beyond boundedness and equicontinuity, the other key ingredient needed for compactness is tightness; see Definition
6.1
.

Proposition 4.6

(Equicontinuity of orbits)
.

Suppose that
Q\subset B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
is equicontinuous in
H^{s}
. Let
H_{1},H_{2}
be Hamiltonians with flows that are continuous on Schwartz class and preserve
A(\varkappa)
for all
|\varkappa|\geq 1
. Then the set

Q^{*}=\left\{e^{J\nabla\left(tH_{1}+\tau H_{2}\right)}q:q\in Q,\,t,\tau\in\mathbb{R},\,\kappa\geq 1\right\}

is equicontinuous in
H^{s}
.

Proof.

By Plancherel (cf.
[
37
, §4]
), it is easy to show that a bounded set
Q\subset H^{s}
is equicontinuous if and only if

\lim\limits_{\kappa\rightarrow+\infty}\sup\limits_{q\in Q}\|q\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}=0.

The result then follows directly from the estimate (
4.12
).
∎

Next we address the question of how
\gamma
,
g_{12}
, and
g_{21}
evolve when taking
A(\kappa)
as the Hamiltonian. As a complex-valued function,
A(\kappa)
cannot be a true Hamiltonian. Nevertheless, there is a natural vector field associated to it by Hamilton’s equations. We caution the reader that this vector field does not respect the relation
r=\pm\bar{q}
. Ultimately, we would like to restrict to the real and imaginary parts of
A(\kappa)
; however, it is convenient to temporarily retain this illusory complex Hamiltonian and recover the real and imaginary parts later using (
4.3
). This context is important for our next two results: the evolution equations we derive for the
A(\kappa)
vector field really represent a complex linear combination of the vector fields associated to the real and imaginary parts (taken separately).

Proposition 4.7

(Lax representation)
.

For distinct
\kappa,\varkappa\in\mathbb{R}\setminus(-1,1)
,

\displaystyle-2(\kappa-\varkappa)\begin{bmatrix}0&\tfrac{\delta A(\kappa)}{\delta r}\\
\tfrac{\delta A(\kappa)}{\delta q}&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=L(\varkappa)\begin{bmatrix}0&\tfrac{\delta A(\kappa)}{\delta r}\\
\tfrac{\delta A(\kappa)}{\delta q}&0\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}0&\tfrac{\delta A(\kappa)}{\delta r}\\
\tfrac{\delta A(\kappa)}{\delta q}&0\end{bmatrix}L(\varkappa)

\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{2}\left[L(\varkappa),\ \begin{bmatrix}\gamma(\kappa)+1&0\\
0&-\gamma(\kappa)-1\end{bmatrix}\right].

Equivalently, under the
A(\kappa)
vector field,
U:=[\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{smallmatrix}]L(\varkappa)
obeys

(4.19)

\displaystyle\frac{d\ }{dt}U=[P,U]\quad\text{with}\quad P=\tfrac{1}{2i(\kappa-\varkappa)}\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{2}(\gamma(\kappa)+1)&-g_{12}(\kappa)\\
g_{21}(\kappa)&-\frac{1}{2}(\gamma(\kappa)+1)\end{bmatrix}.

Proof.

Both identities are elementary computations using (
4.8
) and (
4.4
).
∎

Corollary 4.8
.

Fix distinct
\kappa,\varkappa\in\mathbb{R}\setminus(-1,1)
. Then under the
A(\kappa)
vector field,

(4.20)

\displaystyle i\frac{d}{dt}q=-g_{12}(\kappa)\quad\text{and}\quad i\frac{d}{dt}r=-g_{21}(\kappa).

Moreover,

(4.21)

\displaystyle i\frac{d\ }{dt}g_{12}(\varkappa)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2(\kappa-\varkappa)}\bigl\{[\gamma(\kappa)+1]g_{12}(\varkappa)-g_{12}(\kappa)[\gamma(\varkappa)+1]\bigr\},

\displaystyle i\frac{d\ }{dt}g_{21}(\varkappa)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{-1}{2(\kappa-\varkappa)}\bigl\{[\gamma(\kappa)+1]g_{21}(\varkappa)-g_{21}(\kappa)[\gamma(\varkappa)+1]\bigr\},

and
\partial_{t}\gamma(\varkappa)+\partial_{x}j_{\gamma}(\varkappa,\kappa)=0
where

(4.22)

\displaystyle j_{\gamma}(\varkappa,\kappa):=\tfrac{-i}{2(\kappa-\varkappa)^{2}}\Bigl[g_{12}(\kappa)g_{21}(\varkappa)+g_{12}(\varkappa)g_{21}(\kappa)-\tfrac{\gamma(\kappa)\gamma(\varkappa)+\gamma(\varkappa)+\gamma(\kappa)}{2}\Bigr].

Lastly,
\partial_{t}\rho(\varkappa)+\partial_{x}j(\varkappa,\kappa)=0
with

(4.23)

\displaystyle j(\varkappa,\kappa):=-i\frac{g_{12}(\kappa)g_{21}(\varkappa)+g_{21}(\kappa)g_{12}(\varkappa)}{2(\kappa-\varkappa)(2+\gamma(\varkappa))}+i\frac{\gamma(\kappa)}{4(\kappa-\varkappa)}.

Proof.

The identities (
4.20
) simply recapitulate (
1.2
) and (
4.4
).

Combining (
1.2
), the resolvent identity, and Proposition
4.7
, we have

\displaystyle i\frac{d\ }{dt}G(x,z;\varkappa)

\displaystyle=-\int G(x,y;\varkappa)\begin{bmatrix}0&\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta r}(y)\\
\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta q}(y)&0\end{bmatrix}G(y,z;\varkappa)\,dy

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2(\kappa-\varkappa)}\left(\begin{bmatrix}0&\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta r}(x)\\
\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta q}(x)&0\end{bmatrix}G(x,z;\varkappa)+G(x,z;\varkappa)\begin{bmatrix}0&\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta r}(z)\\
\tfrac{\delta A}{\delta q}(z)&0\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\tfrac{1}{4(\kappa-\varkappa)}[\gamma(x;\kappa)+1]\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{bmatrix}G(x,z;\varkappa)

\displaystyle\qquad-\tfrac{1}{4(\kappa-\varkappa)}G(x,z;\varkappa)[\gamma(z;\kappa)+1]\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{bmatrix}.

This quantity is actually a continuous function of
x
and
z
(as can be seen from the middle expression) and so we may restrict to
z=x
. Thus,
by (
4.4
),

\displaystyle i\tfrac{d\ }{dt}G(x,x;\varkappa)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{-1}{2(\kappa-\varkappa)}\begin{bmatrix}g_{12}(\kappa)g_{21}(\varkappa)-g_{12}(\varkappa)g_{21}(\kappa)\!\!&g_{12}(\kappa)[\gamma(\varkappa)+1]\\
-g_{21}(\kappa)[\gamma(\varkappa)+1]&\!\!g_{12}(\kappa)g_{21}(\varkappa)-g_{12}(\varkappa)g_{21}(\kappa)\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\quad{}+\tfrac{\gamma(\kappa)+1}{2(\kappa-\varkappa)}\begin{bmatrix}0&g_{12}(\varkappa)\\
-g_{21}(\varkappa)&0\end{bmatrix}.

This then yields (
4.21
) directly and (
4.22
) by invoking (
3.14
).

The claim (
4.23
) follows from a lengthy computation using (
4.21
), (
4.22
), (
3.14
), and (
3.31
).
∎

Corollary
4.8
shows that both
\rho(\varkappa)
and
\gamma(\varkappa)
obey microscopic conservation laws. From Lemma
4.1
we see that the corresponding macroscopic conservation laws are
A(\varkappa)
and
\partial_{\varkappa}A(\varkappa)
, respectively; thus, these two microscopic conservation laws are closely related. In the analysis that follows we shall rely exclusively on the conservation law associated to
\rho
, rather than
\gamma
. Let us explain why.

As we saw already in (
1.10
), the quantity
\gamma
arises very naturally in the theory and indeed, it was the basis of our initial investigations of the problem. While it may be possible to build the entire theory around
\gamma
, we can attest that this approach rapidly becomes extremely tiresome. It took us a very long time to discover the density
\rho
that expresses the conservation of
A(\varkappa)
and this innovation has immensely simplified all that follows. A major virtue of
\rho
compared to
\gamma
is coercivity.

The goal of our next lemma is to give a simple expression of this distinction, by looking only at the quadratic terms in the currents associated to the basic Hamiltonians appearing as coefficients in the expansion (
4.7
). In particular, Lemma
4.9
shows that the current
j_{\gamma}
associated with
\gamma
is not coercive under the (
mKdV
) flow.

Note that the terms in the series (
4.7
) are alternately real and imaginary. Correspondingly, to exploit the coercivity of
\operatorname{Im}j
exhibited below, we shall need to use
\operatorname{Im}\rho
when studying (
NLS
) and
\operatorname{Re}\rho
when studying (
mKdV
). It is also instructive to remember that monotone observables must be odd (not even) under time reversal.

The identities (
4.24
) and (
4.25
) appearing in the proof below also show us that neither
\operatorname{Re}j
nor
\operatorname{Re}j_{\gamma}
possess any coercivity.

Lemma 4.9

(Coercivity of the current)
.

The coefficients in the asymptotic series

\displaystyle\int\operatorname{Im}j^{[2]}(\varkappa,\kappa)\,dx=\pm\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\biggl\{\frac{(-1)^{\ell}(2\ell+1)}{(2\kappa)^{2\ell+2}}\varkappa{\mkern 2.0mu}C_{\ell}(\varkappa)+\frac{(-1)^{\ell+1}(\ell+1)}{(2\kappa)^{2\ell+3}}C_{\ell+1}(\varkappa)\biggr\}

are coercive; indeed,

\displaystyle C_{\ell}=\int\frac{2{\mkern 1.0mu}\xi^{2\ell}|\hat{q}(\xi)|^{2}}{4\varkappa^{2}+\xi^{2}}\,d\xi.

The corresponding asymptotic series for
j_{\gamma}
is

\displaystyle\int\operatorname{Im}j_{\gamma}^{[2]}(\varkappa,\kappa)\,dx=\pm\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\biggl\{\frac{(-1)^{\ell}(2\ell+1)}{(2\kappa)^{2\ell+2}}\frac{\partial[\varkappa{\mkern 2.0mu}C_{\ell}(\varkappa)]}{\partial\varkappa}+\frac{(-1)^{\ell+1}(\ell+1)}{(2\kappa)^{2\ell+3}}\frac{\partial C_{\ell+1}(\varkappa)}{\partial\varkappa}\biggr\}.

The coefficients appearing for even powers of
\kappa
are never sign definite; this undermines the utility of
\gamma
.

Proof.

From (
4.23
), we readily find

(4.24)

\displaystyle\int j^{[2]}(\varkappa,\kappa)\,dx=\int\frac{2i\varkappa-\xi}{(2\kappa-i\xi)^{2}}\frac{\hat{q}(\xi)\hat{r}(-\xi)}{4\varkappa^{2}+\xi^{2}}\,d\xi,

from which the expansion is readily verified. The analogous formula for
j_{\gamma}
is

(4.25)

\displaystyle\int j_{\gamma}^{[2]}(\varkappa,\kappa)\,dx=2\int\frac{4\varkappa\xi+i(\xi^{2}-4\varkappa^{2})}{(2\kappa-i\xi)^{2}}\frac{\hat{q}(\xi)\hat{r}(-\xi)}{(4\varkappa^{2}+\xi^{2})^{2}}\,d\xi.

The fact that this coincides with the
\varkappa
-derivative of (
4.24
) is not a coincidence; it reflects the first identity in (
4.5
).

It easy to verify that
\partial_{\varkappa}[\varkappa{\mkern 2.0mu}C_{\ell}(\varkappa)]
is never sign definite because it contains the factor
\xi^{2}-4\varkappa^{2}
.
∎

In view of the asymptotic expansion (
4.7
), Corollary
4.8
provides an efficient method for deriving the evolutions of
g_{12}
and
\gamma
under (
NLS
) and (
mKdV
), although they are also readily computable directly from the definitions.

Corollary 4.10

(Induced flows)
.

Fix
\varkappa\in\mathbb{R}\setminus(-1,1)
.

Under the
M
flow (i.e., phase rotation),

(4.26)

\displaystyle i\frac{d}{dt}g_{12}

\displaystyle=g_{12}\quad\text{and}\quad i\frac{d}{dt}\gamma=0.

Under the
P
flow (i.e., spatial translation),

(4.27)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}g_{12}

\displaystyle=g_{12}^{\prime}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{d}{dt}\gamma=\gamma^{\prime}.

Under the
H_{\mathrm{NLS}}
flow (
NLS
),

(4.28)

\displaystyle i\frac{d}{dt}g_{12}

\displaystyle=-g_{12}^{\prime\prime}+4qrg_{12}+2q^{2}g_{21},

(4.29)

\displaystyle i\frac{d}{dt}\gamma

\displaystyle=\bigl\{2rg_{12}-2qg_{21}-4\varkappa\gamma\bigr\}^{\prime}.

Under the
H_{\mathrm{mKdV}}
flow (
mKdV
),

(4.30)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}g_{12}

\displaystyle=-g_{12}^{\prime\prime\prime}+6qrg_{12}^{\prime}+6qg_{21}q^{\prime}+6g_{12}rq^{\prime},

(4.31)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\gamma

\displaystyle=-\gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}+\bigl\{12\varkappa(rg_{12}-qg_{21})-12\varkappa^{2}\gamma+6qr(1+\gamma)\bigr\}^{\prime}.

These expressions highlight two phenomena that are worthy of note. The first is that the evolution of
\gamma
has the structure of a microscopic conservation law. This has been discussed already, in the context of (
4.22
).

Although rather less obvious, these formulas also show that
g_{12}
obeys the linearized equation around the trajectory
q
. To explain why, let us first consider a generic one-parameter family of solutions
q(t;\tau)
to a given PDE, say (
NLS
). Here
\tau
is the parameter, while
t
is time. Evidently, the parametric derivative of
q
obeys the linearized equation:

i\partial_{t}\tfrac{\partial q}{\partial\tau}=-\partial^{2}_{x}\tfrac{\partial q}{\partial\tau}\pm 4|q|^{2}\tfrac{\partial q}{\partial\tau}\pm 2q^{2}\tfrac{\partial\bar{q}}{\partial\tau}.

This should be compared to (
4.28
), noting the conjugation symmetry (
3.15
).

Finally, to apply this general reasoning to the case at hand, we define our parametric family of solutions to the
H
-flow with initial data
q_{0}
via

q(t;\tau)=\exp\bigl\{tJ\nabla H+\tau J\nabla A(\varkappa)\bigr\}q_{0}

and then apply (
4.4
).

4.1.
Regularized and difference flows

As discussed in the introduction, a key ingredient in our arguments is the decomposition of the full evolution into two commuting parts. The first is a regularized part, that captures the dominant portion of the dynamics, while being very tame at high frequencies. The second part, which we call the difference flow, restores the proper evolution to the high frequencies, but otherwise is very close to the identity.

The starting point for the corresponding decomposition of the Hamiltonian is (
4.7
), which we essentially rearrange to isolate an approximation to the true Hamiltonian. While we wish to consider only real-valued Hamiltonians and taking real and imaginary parts of (
4.7
) is a transparent way to do this, we should also acknowledge a more subtle point: in order to obtain local smoothing for the difference flow, it is essential that the regularized Hamiltonian retains the same conjugation/time-reversal symmetry as the full Hamiltonian.

Definition 4.11
.

Associated to each
\kappa\geq 1
, we define
regularized Hamiltonians

(4.32)

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{\kappa}

\displaystyle=-8\kappa^{3}\operatorname{Re}A(\kappa)+4\kappa^{2}M=-4\kappa^{3}\bigl[A(\kappa)-A(-\kappa)\bigr]+4\kappa^{2}M,

(4.33)

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{mKdV}}^{\kappa}

\displaystyle=16\kappa^{4}\operatorname{Im}A(\kappa)+4\kappa^{2}P=-8i\kappa^{4}\bigl[A(\kappa)+A(-\kappa)\bigr]+4\kappa^{2}P,

as functions on
B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
, as well as
difference Hamiltonians
,

(4.34)

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{\mathrm{diff}}=H_{\mathrm{NLS}}-H_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{\kappa}\quad\text{and}\quad H_{\mathrm{mKdV}}^{\mathrm{diff}}=H_{\mathrm{mKdV}}-H_{\mathrm{mKdV}}^{\kappa}.

One of the key features of the regularized flows is that they are readily seen to be well-posed:

Proposition 4.12

(Global well-posedness of the regularized flows)
.

There exists
\delta>0
so that for all
\kappa\geq 1
the
H_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{\kappa}
and
H_{\mathrm{mKdV}}^{\kappa}
flows

(NLS
κ
)

\displaystyle i\frac{d}{dt}q

\displaystyle=4\kappa^{3}\left(g_{12}(\kappa)-g_{12}(-\kappa)\right)+4\kappa^{2}q,

(mKdV
κ
)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}q

\displaystyle=8\kappa^{4}\left(g_{12}(\kappa)+g_{12}(-\kappa)\right)+4\kappa^{2}q^{\prime}

are globally well-posed for initial data in
B_{\delta}
. These solutions conserve
\alpha(\varkappa)
for every
\varkappa\geq 1
. Moreover, if the initial data is Schwartz then so are the corresponding solutions.

Proof.

The evolution equations follow directly from (
4.32
) and (
4.33
) by applying (
4.3
) and (
4.4
).

Using the diffeomorphism property of the map
q\mapsto g_{12}(\kappa)
proved in Proposition
3.2
, we may view the equations (
NLSκ
) and (
mKdVκ
) as ODEs in
H^{s}
, the latter after making the change of variables

(t,x)\mapsto(t,x-4\kappa^{2}t).

Local well-posedness then follows from the Picard-Lindelöf Theorem. Further, as the map
q\mapsto g_{12}(\kappa)
preserves the Schwartz class, it is clear that if
q(0)\in B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
then the corresponding solution remains Schwartz. Finally, to extend the solution globally in time we first observe that for
q(0)\in B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
we may apply Lemma
4.3
to deduce the conservation of
\alpha(\varkappa)
for all
\varkappa\geq 1
. Applying Proposition
4.4
we may then extend the solution globally in time for
q(0)\in B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
and then for all
q(0)\in B_{\delta}
by approximation.
∎

From Lemma
4.3
we see that the full and regularized Hamiltonian evolutions commute (at least on Schwartz space). This allows us to obtain evolution equations for the difference Hamiltonians by simply combining the corresponding vector fields. In this way, Proposition
4.12
together with Corollaries
4.8
and
4.10
yields the following:

Corollary 4.13

(Difference flows)
.

Consider any
\kappa,\varkappa\geq 1
and any initial data in
B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
. Under the NLS difference flow,

(NLS-diff)

\displaystyle i\frac{d}{dt}q=-q^{\prime\prime}+2q^{2}r-4\kappa^{3}\left(g_{12}(\kappa)-g_{12}(-\kappa)\right)-4\kappa^{2}q,

(4.35)

\displaystyle\left\{\qquad\begin{aligned} i\frac{d}{dt}g_{12}(\varkappa)&=-g_{12}(\varkappa)^{\prime\prime}+4qrg_{12}(\varkappa)+2q^{2}g_{21}(\varkappa)\\
&\quad+\frac{2\kappa^{3}}{\kappa-\varkappa}\bigl\{[\gamma(\kappa)+1]g_{12}(\varkappa)-g_{12}(\kappa)[\gamma(\varkappa)+1]\bigr\}\\
&\quad+\frac{2\kappa^{3}}{\kappa+\varkappa}\bigl\{[\gamma(-\kappa)+1]g_{12}(\varkappa)-g_{12}(-\kappa)[\gamma(\varkappa)+1]\bigr\}\\
&\quad-4\kappa^{2}g_{12}(\varkappa),\end{aligned}\right.

and under the mKdV difference flow

(mKdV-diff)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}q=-q^{\prime\prime\prime}+6qrq^{\prime}-8\kappa^{4}\left(g_{12}(\kappa)+g_{12}(-\kappa)\right)-4\kappa^{2}q^{\prime},

(4.36)

\displaystyle\left\{\qquad\begin{aligned} \frac{d}{dt}g_{12}(\varkappa)&=-g_{12}(\varkappa)^{\prime\prime\prime}+6qrg_{12}(\varkappa)^{\prime}+6qq^{\prime}g_{21}(\varkappa)+6rq^{\prime}g_{12}(\varkappa)\\
&\quad+\frac{4\kappa^{4}}{\kappa-\varkappa}\bigl\{[\gamma(\kappa)+1]g_{12}(\varkappa)-g_{12}(\kappa)[\gamma(\varkappa)+1]\bigr\}\\
&\quad-\frac{4\kappa^{4}}{\kappa+\varkappa}\bigl\{[\gamma(-\kappa)+1]g_{12}(\varkappa)-g_{12}(-\kappa)[\gamma(\varkappa)+1]\bigr\}\\
&\quad-4\kappa^{2}g_{12}(\varkappa)^{\prime}.\end{aligned}\right.

We end this section with the following result, which encapsulates the microscopic conservation law attendant to
A(\varkappa)
under the various flows considered in this paper.

Corollary 4.14
.

For
\kappa,\varkappa\geq 1
and initial data in
B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
, we have

(4.37)

\partial_{t}\rho(\varkappa)+\partial_{x}j_{\star}=0,

for each of the NLS, mKdV, and difference flows; the currents are given by

\displaystyle j_{\mathrm{NLS}}(\varkappa)=-i\left(\tfrac{q^{\prime}\cdot g_{21}(\varkappa)+r^{\prime}\cdot g_{12}(\varkappa)}{2+\gamma(\varkappa)}-qr+2\varkappa\rho(\varkappa)\right),

\displaystyle j_{\mathrm{mKdV}}(\varkappa)=\tfrac{(q^{\prime\prime}-2q^{2}r)\cdot g_{21}(\varkappa)-(r^{\prime\prime}-2r^{2}q)\cdot g_{12}(\varkappa)}{2+\gamma(\varkappa)}-q^{\prime}r+qr^{\prime}+2i\varkappa j_{\mathrm{NLS}}(\varkappa),

\displaystyle j_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(\varkappa,\kappa)=j_{\mathrm{NLS}}(\varkappa)+4\kappa^{3}\left(j(\varkappa,\kappa)-j(\varkappa,-\kappa)\right),

\displaystyle j_{\mathrm{mKdV}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(\varkappa,\kappa)=j_{\mathrm{mKdV}}(\varkappa)+8i\kappa^{4}\left(j(\varkappa,\kappa)+j(\varkappa,-\kappa)\right)+4\kappa^{2}\rho(\varkappa).

5.
Local smoothing

The goal of this section is to prove local smoothing estimates, not only for the NLS and mKdV flows, but also for the difference flows. To do this, we will be using an integrated form of the microscopic conservation law (
4.37
) for
A(\varkappa)
:

(5.1)

\int_{-1}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}j_{\star}(t,x;\varkappa)\,\psi_{h}^{12}(x)\,dx\,dt=\int\big[\rho(1,x;\varkappa)-\rho(-1,x;\varkappa)\big]\,\Psi_{h}(x)\,dx,

where
h\in\mathbb{R}
is a translation parameter,
\psi_{h}
is as in (
1.5
),

(5.2)

\Psi_{h}(x):=\int_{-\infty}^{x}\psi_{h}^{12}(y)\,dy,

and the currents are as recorded in Corollary
4.14
.

Eventually, we will take a supremum over
h\in\mathbb{R}
as in (
1.6
). With this in mind, implicit constants in this section are always to be interpreted as independent of
h
.

Control of the local smoothing norm will originate in the coercivity of the LHS(
5.1
) that we have already hinted at in Lemma
4.9
. The first result in this section, Lemma
5.1
, shows that this coercivity of the quadratic currents survives in the presence of localization. As noted already in Section
4
, we will need to take the real or imaginary part of (
5.1
), depending on the flow in question.

To continue, we will show how to control the RHS(
5.1
); see Lemma
5.2
. This leaves us to control the higher order terms in the currents; this is the topic of Lemma
5.3
. In estimating such terms, it is convenient to combine the key norms:

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{NLS}_{\kappa}}^{2}:=\|q\|_{X_{\kappa}^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}+\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{mKdV}_{\kappa}}^{2}:=\|q\|_{X_{\kappa}^{s+1}}^{2}+\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}^{2},

with the convention that a missing subscript means
\kappa=1
.

The proofs of Lemmas
5.1
and
5.3
are both quite substantial. With this in mind, we delay presenting these proofs until after giving the main results of this section, namely, Propositions
5.4
,
5.5
,
5.6
, and
5.8
.

Lemma 5.1

(Estimates for
j_{\star}^{[2]}
)
.

Fix
\delta>0
sufficiently small. Then

(5.3)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}\int j_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{[2]}(\varkappa)\,\psi_{h}^{12}\,dx

\displaystyle=\pm 2\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}+\mathcal{O}\Big(\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\Big),

(5.4)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Re}\int j_{\mathrm{mKdV}}^{[2]}(\varkappa)\,\psi_{h}^{12}\,dx

\displaystyle=\mp 6\varkappa\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{O}\Big(\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}+\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\Big),

uniformly for
q\in B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
,
\varkappa\geq 1
, and
h\in\mathbb{R}
. Analogously,

(5.5)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Im}\int j_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{\mathrm{diff}}{}^{[2]}(\varkappa,\kappa)\,\psi_{h}^{12}\,dx

\displaystyle=\pm 2\int\frac{\xi^{4}(8\kappa^{2}+\xi^{2})|\widehat{\psi_{h}^{6}q}(\xi)|^{2}\,d\xi}{(4\varkappa^{2}+\xi^{2})(4\kappa^{2}+\xi^{2})^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\Big(\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\Big),\!\!\!

(5.6)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Re}\int j_{\mathrm{mKdV}}^{\mathrm{diff}\;[2]}(\varkappa,\kappa)\,\psi_{h}^{12}\,dx

\displaystyle=\mp 2\varkappa\int\frac{(20\kappa^{2}+3\xi^{2})\xi^{4}|\widehat{\psi_{h}^{6}q}(\xi)|^{2}\,d\xi}{(4\varkappa^{2}+\xi^{2})(4\kappa^{2}+\xi^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{O}\Big(\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}+\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\Big),

uniformly for
q\in B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
,
\kappa,\varkappa\geq 1
, and
h\in\mathbb{R}
.

Lemma 5.2

(Estimate for
\rho
)
.

Let
q\in B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
and
\Psi_{h}
be defined as in (
5.2
). Then for
\varkappa\geq 1
we have the estimate

(5.7)

\left|\int\rho(\varkappa)\,\Psi_{h}\,dx\right|\lesssim\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}+\varkappa^{-2(2s+1)}\delta^{2}\|q\|_{H^{s}}^{2},

where the implicit constant is independent of
h,\varkappa
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
4.4
, we write
\rho(\varkappa)=\rho^{[2]}(\varkappa)+\rho^{[\geq 4]}(\varkappa)
. From (
4.13
), we bound

\left|\int\rho^{[2]}(\varkappa)\,\Psi_{h}\,dx\right|\lesssim\|\Psi_{h}q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\lesssim\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}.

Using (
4.14
) and (
4.17
), we may bound

\left|\int\rho^{[\geq 4]}(\varkappa)\,\Psi_{h}\,dx\right|\lesssim\varkappa^{-2(2s+1)}\delta^{2}\|q\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

To control the contribution of the remaining part
j_{\star}^{[\geq 4]}
of the current, we use the following lemma. The proof of this result will take up the majority of this section.

Lemma 5.3

(Estimates for
j_{\star}^{[\geq 4]}
)
.

Let
q\in\mathcal{C}([-1,1];B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S})
with
\delta>0
sufficiently small. For any
\varkappa\geq 1
, we have

(5.8)

\displaystyle\left\|\int j_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{[\geq 4]}(\varkappa)\,\psi_{h}^{12}\,dx\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varkappa^{-2(2s+1)}\delta^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{2},

(5.9)

\displaystyle\left\|\int j_{\mathrm{mKdV}}^{[\geq 4]}(\varkappa)\,\psi_{h}^{12}\,dx\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big[\varkappa^{-1}+\varkappa^{-2(2s+1)}\log^{4}|2\varkappa|\big]\delta^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{mKdV}}^{2}.

Moreover, if
\kappa\geq 8
and
\varkappa\in[\kappa^{\frac{2}{3}},\frac{1}{2}\kappa]\cup[2\kappa,\infty)
, then

(5.10)

\displaystyle\left\|\int\!j_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{\mathrm{diff}}{}^{[\geq 4]}(\varkappa,\kappa)\,\psi_{h}^{12}\,dx\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigl[\tfrac{\kappa}{\kappa+\varkappa}\kappa^{-\frac{4}{3}(2s+1)}+\varkappa^{-2(2s+1)}\bigr]\delta^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{NLS}_{\kappa}}^{2},

whereas for
\varkappa\in[\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}},\frac{1}{2}\kappa]\cup[2\kappa,\infty)
, we have

(5.11)

\displaystyle\left\|\int\!j_{\mathrm{mKdV}}^{\mathrm{diff}\;[\geq 4]}(\varkappa,\kappa)\,\psi_{h}^{12}\,dx\right\|_{L^{1}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigl[\tfrac{\kappa}{\kappa+\varkappa}\kappa^{-(2s+1)}+\varkappa^{-2(2s+1)}\log|2\varkappa|\bigr]\delta^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{mKdV}_{\kappa}}^{2}.

In all cases, the implicit constant is independent of
h
,
\varkappa
, and
\kappa
.

The restriction
\kappa\geq 8
(rather than
\kappa\geq 1
) appearing in this proposition is imposed to avoid confusion in the meaning of the constraints on
\varkappa
. It guarantees that in both cases, the first interval is nonempty.

The fact that the
\kappa=1
case of (
5.11
) yields a better bound than (
5.9
) warrants explanation. Ultimately, this is because LHS(
5.11
) requires a much more detailed analysis in order to achieve a satisfactory bound. The bound (
5.9
) could be improved by a parallel analysis; however, this is not needed for what follows.

With these estimates in hand we are now able to prove our local smoothing estimates:

Proposition 5.4

(Local smoothing for the NLS)
.

There exists
\delta>0
so that for any
q(0)\in B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
, the solution
q(t)
of (
NLS
) satisfies the estimate

(5.12)

\|q\|_{X^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.

Further, we have the high frequency estimate

(5.13)

\bigl\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{\kappa}^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}+\kappa^{-(2s+1)}\delta^{2}\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2},

uniformly for
\kappa\geq 1
.

Proof.

Consider the imaginary part of (
5.1
). Applying the estimates (
5.3
) and (
5.8
) to the LHS and the estimate (
5.7
) to the RHS, we obtain

\displaystyle\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}+\varkappa^{-2(2s+1)}\delta^{2}\left(\|q\|_{X^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}+\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}^{2}\right),

where the implicit constant is independent of
h,\varkappa
. We then choose
-\frac{1}{2}<s^{\prime}<s
and apply the a priori estimate (
4.12
) to obtain

\displaystyle\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varkappa^{-(2s^{\prime}+1)}\|q(0)\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}+\varkappa^{-2(2s+1)}\delta^{2}\left(\|q\|_{X^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}+\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\right).

Taking
\kappa\geq 1
and using (
2.7
), we obtain

(5.14)

\displaystyle\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}_{\kappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\approx\int_{\kappa}^{\infty}\varkappa^{2s+1}\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\,\tfrac{d\varkappa}{\varkappa}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}+\kappa^{-(2s+1)}\delta^{2}\left(\|q\|_{X^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}+\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\right).

To complete the proof, we take
\kappa=1
to deduce

\displaystyle\|\psi_{h}^{6}q\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|P_{>1}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}+\|P_{\leq 1}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{2}\|q\|_{X^{s+\frac{1}{2}}}^{2}+\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.

Taking the supremum over
h\in\mathbb{R}
and choosing
0<\delta\ll 1
sufficiently small, we obtain the estimate (
5.12
). The claim (
5.13
) then follows from (
5.12
) and (
5.14
).
∎

An essentially identical argument yields the corresponding result for the mKdV:

Proposition 5.5

(Local smoothing for the mKdV)
.

There exists
\delta>0
so that for any
q(0)\in B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
, the solution
q(t)
of (
mKdV
) satisfies the estimate

(5.15)

\|q\|_{X^{s+1}}^{2}\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.

Further, we have the high frequency estimate

(5.16)

\bigl\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{\kappa}^{s}}^{2}\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}+\kappa^{-(2s+1)}\log^{4}\!|2\kappa|\,\delta^{2}\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2},

uniformly for
\kappa\geq 1
.

Proof.

Consider the real part of (
5.1
). Applying (
5.4
) and (
5.9
) to the LHS and applying (
5.7
) to the RHS, we deduce that

\displaystyle\varkappa\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}+(1+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon})\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\big[\varkappa^{-1}+\varkappa^{-2(2s+1)}\log^{4}\!|2\varkappa|\big]\delta^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{mKdV}}^{2}

for any
0<\varepsilon<1
. Here the implicit constant is independent of
h,\varkappa,\varepsilon
. Applying the a priori estimate (
4.12
), for any
-\frac{1}{2}<s^{\prime}<s
we obtain

\displaystyle\varkappa\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}+(1+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon})\varkappa^{-(2s^{\prime}+1)}\|q(0)\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\big[\varkappa^{-1}+\varkappa^{-2(2s+1)}\log^{4}\!|2\varkappa|\big]\delta^{2}\Big(\|q\|_{X^{s+1}}^{2}+\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\Big).

Using the estimate (
2.7
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\approx\int_{\kappa}^{\infty}\varkappa^{2s+2}\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\,\tfrac{d\varkappa}{\varkappa}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|(\psi_{h}q)^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}+(1+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon})\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\big[\kappa^{2s}+\kappa^{-(2s+1)}\log^{4}\!|2\kappa|\big]\delta^{2}\Big(\|q\|_{X^{s+1}}^{2}+\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\Big).

Choosing
0<\varepsilon<1
sufficiently small to defeat the implicit constant, we get

(5.17)

\displaystyle\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}+\big[\kappa^{2s}+\kappa^{-(2s+1)}\log^{4}\!|2\kappa|\big]\delta^{2}\Big(\|q\|_{X^{s+1}}^{2}+\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\Big).

To complete the proof, we apply the estimate (
5.17
) with
\kappa=1
to bound

\displaystyle\|\psi_{h}^{6}q\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s+1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|P_{>1}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s}}^{2}+\|P_{\leq 1}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{2}\|q\|_{X^{s+1}}^{2}+\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.

Taking the supremum over
h\in\mathbb{R}
and choosing
0<\delta\ll 1
sufficiently small, we obtain (
5.15
). The estimate (
5.16
) then follows from (
5.15
), (
5.17
), and the observation that
\kappa^{2s}\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}_{\kappa}}^{2}
.
∎

In Propositions
5.4
and
5.5
, the parameter
\kappa
plays the role of a frequency threshold. The fact that we obtain decay as
\kappa\to\infty
will be essential both for proving tightness and for proving that the data-to-solution map is continuous in the local smoothing norm.

We now turn to proving local smoothing for the difference flows. In this context,
\kappa
takes on a new meaning as the parameter appearing in the regularized Hamiltonians; see (
4.34
). In this role,
\kappa
marks a border (in frequency space): it is only for frequencies below
\kappa
that the regularized and full Hamiltonian flows well-approximate one another. Correspondingly, it is only for frequencies above
\kappa
that we can expect to recover the full local smoothing effects documented above for (
NLS
) and (
mKdV
).

Proposition 5.6

(Local smoothing for the NLS difference flow)
.

There exists
\delta>0
so that for any
q(0)\in B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
and
\kappa\geq 8
, the solution
q(t)
of the NLS difference flow (
NLS-diff
) with parameter
\kappa
satisfies the estimate

(5.18)

\|q\|_{X^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\kappa}}^{2}\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2},

where the implicit constant is independent of
\kappa
.

Proof.

Let us write
I=[\kappa^{\frac{2}{3}},\frac{1}{2}\kappa]\cup[2\kappa,\infty)
, which is the region of
\varkappa
over which the estimate (
5.10
) will be proved.

Taking the imaginary part of (
5.1
) and applying (
5.5
), (
5.10
), and (
5.7
), we find

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}+\bigl[\tfrac{\kappa}{\kappa+\varkappa}\kappa^{-\frac{4}{3}(2s+1)}+\varkappa^{-2(2s+1)}\bigr]\delta^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{NLS}_{\kappa}}^{2},

uniformly for
\varkappa\in I
. Choosing
-\frac{1}{2}<s^{\prime}<s
and employing the a priori estimate (
4.12
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varkappa^{-(2s^{\prime}+1)}\|q(0)\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}+\bigl[\tfrac{\kappa}{\kappa+\varkappa}\kappa^{-\frac{4}{3}(2s+1)}+\varkappa^{-2(2s+1)}\bigr]\delta^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{NLS}_{\kappa}}^{2},

uniformly for
\varkappa\in I
. Next we wish to integrate out
\varkappa
.

By Lemma
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}_{\kappa^{2/3}}}^{2}\approx\int_{\kappa^{\frac{2}{3}}}^{\infty}\varkappa^{2s+1}\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\tfrac{d\varkappa}{\varkappa},

from which it follows that

(5.19)

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}_{\kappa^{2/3}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\approx\int_{I}\varkappa^{2s+1}\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\tfrac{d\varkappa}{\varkappa},

because the integrand on the interval
[\kappa/2,2\kappa]
is comparable to that on
[2\kappa,4\kappa]
.

Proceeding in this way, we find that

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}_{\kappa^{2/3}}}^{2}\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\kappa^{-\frac{1}{3}(1+2s)}\delta^{2}\Big(\|q\|_{X^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\kappa}}^{2}+\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\Big).

To complete the proof, we decompose

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{\psi_{h}^{6}q}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s+\frac{3}{2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}P_{\leq\kappa^{\frac{2}{3}}}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s+\frac{3}{2}}}^{2}+\|\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}P_{>\kappa^{\frac{2}{3}}}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s+\frac{3}{2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}_{\kappa^{2/3}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\delta^{2}\|q\|_{X^{s+\frac{1}{2}}_{\kappa}}^{2}.

Taking the supremum over
h\in\mathbb{R}
, we obtain the estimate (
5.18
) whenever
0<\delta\ll 1
is sufficiently small, depending only on
s
.
∎

Next, we record a corollary of Proposition
5.6
, which will be used in Section
7
.

Corollary 5.7
.

There exists
\delta>0
so that for any
q(0)\in B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
and
\kappa\geq 8
, the solution
q(t)
of the NLS difference flow (
NLS-diff
) with parameter
\kappa
satisfies

(5.20)

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in\mathbb{R}}\|P_{N}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)\|_{L_{t,x}^{2}}\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}\begin{cases}N^{-s}\quad&\text{if \, $N\leq\kappa^{\frac{2}{3}}$,}\\
\kappa N^{-(\frac{3}{2}+s)}\quad&\text{if \, $\kappa^{\frac{2}{3}}\leq N\leq\kappa$},\\
N^{-(\frac{1}{2}+s)}\quad&\text{if \, $N\geq\kappa$},\\
\end{cases}

uniformly for
N\geq 1
and
\kappa\geq 1
. Consequently,

(5.21)

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl\|\psi_{h}^{6}\tfrac{\partial^{\ell}q}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}\bigr\|_{L_{t,x}^{2}}+\sup_{h\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl\|\tfrac{\partial^{\ell}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}\bigr\|_{L_{t,x}^{2}}\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}\begin{cases}\kappa^{-2+\frac{2}{3}(l-s)}\quad&\text{if \, $\ell=0,1$,}\\
\kappa^{-(\frac{1}{2}+s)}\quad&\text{if \, $\ell=2$},\end{cases}

uniformly for
\kappa\geq 1
.

Proof.

The claim (
5.20
) follows immediately from (
5.18
) and Bernstein inequalities. To obtain (
5.21
), we decompose into Littlewood–Paley pieces, use (
5.20
) and Lemma
2.8
, and then sum.
∎

Proposition 5.8

(Local smoothing for the mKdV difference flow)
.

There exists
\delta>0
so that for any
q(0)\in B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
and
\kappa\geq 8
the solution
q(t)
of the mKdV difference flow (
mKdV-diff
) with parameter
\kappa
satisfies

(5.22)

\|q\|_{X^{s+1}_{\kappa}}^{2}\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2},

where the implicit constant is independent of
\kappa
.

Proof.

Consider the real part of (
5.1
). Applying the estimates (
5.6
), (
5.11
), and (
5.7
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\varkappa\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}+(1+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon})\|q\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\ +\bigl[\tfrac{\kappa}{\kappa+\varkappa}\kappa^{-(2s+1)}+\varkappa^{-2(2s+1)}\log|2\varkappa|\bigr]\delta^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{mKdV}_{\kappa}}^{2},

uniformly for
0<\varepsilon<1
and
\varkappa\in I:=[\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}},\frac{1}{2}\kappa]\cup[2\kappa,\infty)
.

Choosing
-\frac{1}{2}<s^{\prime}<s
and applying the a priori estimate (
4.12
), this becomes

\displaystyle\varkappa\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}+\varkappa^{-(2s^{\prime}+1)}(1+\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon})\|q(0)\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\ +\bigl[\tfrac{\kappa}{\kappa+\varkappa}\kappa^{-(2s+1)}+\varkappa^{-2(2s+1)}\log|2\varkappa|\bigr]\delta^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{mKdV}_{\kappa}}^{2}.

Next we wish to integrate over
\varkappa\in I
. Using Lemma
2.1
as in the proof of Proposition
5.6
, we obtain the following analogues of (
5.19
):

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s}_{\kappa^{1/2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\approx\int_{I}\varkappa^{2s+2}\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\tfrac{d\varkappa}{\varkappa}\approx\int_{I}\varkappa^{2s+1}\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\tfrac{d\varkappa}{\varkappa}.

Proceeding in this way, and choosing
0<\varepsilon<1
sufficiently small, we obtain

\bigl\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\bigr\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s}_{\kappa^{1/2}}}^{2}\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\delta^{2}\|q\|_{X^{s+1}_{\kappa}}^{2}.

To complete the proof, we decompose

\displaystyle\|\tfrac{\psi_{h}^{6}q}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s+2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}P_{\leq\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s+2}}^{2}+\|\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}P_{>\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s+2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s}_{\kappa^{1/2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\delta^{2}\|q\|_{X^{s+1}_{\kappa}}^{2}.

Taking the supremum over
h\in\mathbb{R}
we obtain the estimate (
5.18
) whenever
0<\delta\ll 1
is sufficiently small, depending only on
s
.
∎

Proposition
5.8
directly yields the following analogue of Corollary
5.7
:

Corollary 5.9
.

There exists
\delta>0
so that for any
q(0)\in B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S}
and
\kappa\geq 8
, the solution
q(t)
of the mKdV difference flow (
mKdV-diff
) with parameter
\kappa
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in\mathbb{R}}\|P_{N}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)\|_{L_{t,x}^{2}}\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}\begin{cases}N^{-s}\quad&\text{if \, $N\leq\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}$,}\\
\kappa N^{-(2+s)}\quad&\text{if \, $\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq N\leq\kappa$},\\
N^{-(1+s)}\quad&\text{if \, $N\geq\kappa$},\\
\end{cases}

uniformly for
N\geq 1
and
\kappa\geq 8
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl\|\psi_{h}^{6}\tfrac{\partial^{\ell}q}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}\bigr\|_{L_{t,x}^{2}}+\sup_{h\in\mathbb{R}}\bigl\|\tfrac{\partial^{\ell}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}\bigr\|_{L_{t,x}^{2}}\lesssim\|q(0)\|_{H^{s}}\begin{cases}\kappa^{-2+\frac{1}{2}(l-s)}\quad&\text{if \, $\ell=0,1$,}\\
\kappa^{-(1+s)}\quad&\text{if \, $\ell=2$},\end{cases}

uniformly for
\kappa\geq 2
.

We now turn to the proof of Lemma
5.1
:

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

We introduce the paraproduct
\mathbf{R}[q,r]
with symbol

R(\xi,\eta)=\tfrac{1}{2(2\varkappa-i\xi)}+\tfrac{1}{2(2\varkappa+i\eta)}

so that by (
4.13
) we may write

\rho^{[2]}(x;\varkappa)=\mathbf{R}[q,r](x)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int R(\xi,\eta)\hat{q}(\xi)\hat{r}(\eta)e^{ix(\xi+\eta)}\,d\xi\,d\eta.

We then observe that the quadratic part of the current
j(\varkappa,\kappa)
defined in (
4.23
) may be written as

j^{[2]}(\varkappa,\kappa)=i\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q}{2\kappa-\partial},\tfrac{r}{2\kappa+\partial}].

Expanding in powers of
\kappa
, we readily obtain the expressions

\displaystyle j_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{[2]}(\varkappa)

\displaystyle=i\Big(\mathbf{R}[q,r^{\prime}]-\mathbf{R}[q^{\prime},r]\Big),

\displaystyle j_{\mathrm{mKdV}}^{[2]}(\varkappa)

\displaystyle=\bigl(\mathbf{R}[q,r]\bigr)^{\prime\prime}-3\mathbf{R}[q^{\prime},r^{\prime}],

for the NLS and mKdV flows, as well as the expressions

\displaystyle j_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{\mathrm{diff}}{}^{[2]}(\varkappa,\kappa)

\displaystyle=i\Big(\mathbf{R}[q,r^{\prime}]-\mathbf{R}[q^{\prime},r]\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-16\kappa^{4}i\Big(\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{r^{\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]-\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q^{\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{r}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\Big),

\displaystyle j_{\mathrm{mKdV}}^{\mathrm{diff}}{}^{[2]}(\varkappa,\kappa)

\displaystyle=\bigl(\mathbf{R}[q,r]\bigr)^{\prime\prime}-3\mathbf{R}[q^{\prime},r^{\prime}]

\displaystyle\quad-16\kappa^{4}\big(\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{r}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\bigr)^{\prime\prime}+48\kappa^{4}\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q^{\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{r^{\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]

\displaystyle\quad+4\kappa^{2}\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{r^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]

for the corresponding difference flows. (Alternatively, we may use the definition of the currents from Corollary
4.14
to compute the quadratic components directly.)

If we could simply replace
q,r
by
\psi_{h}^{6}q,\psi_{h}^{6}r
in these expressions, rather than integrating them against
\psi_{h}^{12}
, then we would obtain the leading order terms in (
5.3
)–(
5.6
). Thus, the focal point of our analysis will be bounding the various commutator terms that arise.

Proof of (
5.3
).

Using the above expression we may write

\displaystyle\mathrm{LHS}\eqref{jNLS-quad}

\displaystyle=\pm 2\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}+\operatorname{Re}\int\Big(\mathbf{R}[q,r^{\prime}]\,\psi_{h}^{12}-\mathbf{R}[\psi_{h}^{6}q,(\psi_{h}^{6}r)^{\prime}]\Big)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad-\operatorname{Re}\int\Big(\mathbf{R}[q^{\prime},r]\,\psi_{h}^{12}-\mathbf{R}[(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime},\psi_{h}^{6}r]\Big)\,dx.

By symmetry it suffices to bound

\displaystyle\int\Big(\mathbf{R}[q,r^{\prime}]\,\psi_{h}^{12}-\mathbf{R}[\psi_{h}^{6}q,(\psi_{h}^{6}r)^{\prime}]\Big)\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad=\int[\psi_{h}^{6},\tfrac{1}{2(2\varkappa-\partial)}]q\cdot\psi_{h}^{6}r^{\prime}\,dx-\int\tfrac{1}{2(2\varkappa-\partial)}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)\cdot(\psi_{h}^{6})^{\prime}r\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\int\psi_{h}^{6}q\cdot[\psi_{h}^{6},\tfrac{\partial}{2(2\varkappa+\partial)}]r\,dx,

which may be bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\int\Big(\mathbf{R}[q,r^{\prime}]\,\psi_{h}^{12}-\mathbf{R}[\psi_{h}^{6}q,(\psi_{h}^{6}r)^{\prime}]\Big)\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\|[\psi_{h}^{6},\tfrac{1}{2(2\varkappa-\partial)}]q\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\|\psi_{h}^{6}r^{\prime}\|_{H^{-\frac{3}{2}}_{\varkappa}}+\|\tfrac{1}{2(2\varkappa-\partial)}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\|(\psi_{h}^{6})^{\prime}r\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\|\psi_{h}^{6}q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\|[\psi_{h}^{6},\tfrac{\partial}{2(2\varkappa+\partial)}]r\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2},

as required.

Proof of (
5.5
).

We observe that the difference

j_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{[2]}-j_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{\mathrm{diff}}{}^{[2]}

has an identical expression to
j_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{[2]}
with
q
replaced by
\tfrac{4\kappa^{2}q}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}
. The estimate (
5.5
) then follows from the estimate (
5.3
) and the estimates

\|\tfrac{4\kappa^{2}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\lesssim\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\partial[\psi_{h}^{6},\tfrac{4\kappa^{2}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\lesssim\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}.

Proof of (
5.4
).

Integrating by parts we find

\displaystyle\mathrm{LHS}\eqref{jmKdV-quad}

\displaystyle=\mp 6\varkappa\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{H^{-1}_{\varkappa}}^{2}+\operatorname{Re}\int\mathbf{R}[q,r]\,(\psi_{h}^{12})^{\prime\prime}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad-3\operatorname{Re}\int\Big(\mathbf{R}[q^{\prime},r^{\prime}]\,\psi_{h}^{12}-\mathbf{R}[(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime},(\psi_{h}^{6}r)^{\prime}]\Big)\,dx.

As in the proof of Lemma
5.2
, the second term may be readily bounded by

\left|\int\mathbf{R}[q,r]\,(\psi_{h}^{12})^{\prime\prime}\,dx\right|\lesssim\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}.

For the remaining term we write

\displaystyle\int\Big(\mathbf{R}[q^{\prime},r^{\prime}]\,\psi_{h}^{12}-\mathbf{R}[(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime},(\psi_{h}^{6}r)^{\prime}]\Big)\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad=\int[\psi_{h}^{6},\tfrac{\partial}{2(2\varkappa-\partial)}]q\cdot(\psi_{h}^{6}r)^{\prime}\,dx-\int\tfrac{\partial}{2(2\varkappa-\partial)}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)\cdot(\psi_{h}^{6})^{\prime}r\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\int[\psi_{h}^{6},\tfrac{\partial}{2(2\varkappa-\partial)}]q\cdot(\psi_{h}^{6})^{\prime}r\,dx+\int(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\cdot[\psi_{h}^{6},\tfrac{\partial}{2(2\varkappa+\partial)}]r\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\int(\psi_{h}^{6})^{\prime}q\cdot\tfrac{\partial}{2(2\varkappa+\partial)}(\psi_{h}^{6}r)\,dx-\int(\psi_{h}^{6})^{\prime}q\cdot[\psi_{h}^{6},\tfrac{\partial}{2(2\varkappa+\partial)}]r\,dx.

The first three summands here may be bounded in magnitude via

\displaystyle\|[\psi_{h}^{6},\tfrac{\partial}{2(2\varkappa-\partial)}]q\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\|(\psi_{h}^{6}r)^{\prime}\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}+\|\tfrac{\partial}{2(2\varkappa-\partial)}

\displaystyle(\psi_{h}^{6}q)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\|(\psi_{h}^{6})^{\prime}r\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\|(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime}\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}

and

\displaystyle\|[\psi_{h}^{6},\tfrac{\partial}{2(2\varkappa-\partial)}]q\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\|(\psi_{h}^{6})^{\prime}r\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2},

both of which are acceptable. The remaining three summands can then be estimated in a parallel fashion; indeed, this is tantamount to replacing
\varkappa
by
-\varkappa
.

Proof of (
5.6
).

Integrating by parts several times we obtain the identity

\displaystyle\mathrm{LHS}\eqref{jmKdVdiff-quad}

\displaystyle=\mp 2\varkappa\int\tfrac{(20\kappa^{2}+3\xi^{2})\xi^{4}}{(4\varkappa^{2}+\xi^{2})(4\kappa^{2}+\xi^{2})^{2}}|\widehat{\psi_{h}^{6}q}|^{2}\,d\xi

\displaystyle\quad-3\operatorname{Re}\int\Big(\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q^{\prime\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\,\psi_{h}^{12}-\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}r)^{\prime\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\Big)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad-20\kappa^{2}\operatorname{Re}\int\Big(\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{r^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\,\psi_{h}^{12}-\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}r)^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\Big)\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+\operatorname{Re}\int\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{r^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\,(\psi_{h}^{12})^{\prime\prime}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad+20\kappa^{2}\operatorname{Re}\int\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q^{\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{r^{\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\,(\psi_{h}^{12})^{\prime\prime}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad-4\kappa^{2}\operatorname{Re}\int\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{r}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\,(\psi_{h}^{12})^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\,dx.

The final three terms are lower order errors that may be bounded by

\displaystyle\left|\int\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{r^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\,(\psi_{h}^{12})^{\prime\prime}\,dx\right|\lesssim\|\tfrac{q^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\lesssim\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}

\displaystyle\left|\kappa^{2}\int\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q^{\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{r^{\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\,(\psi_{h}^{12})^{\prime\prime}\,dx\right|\lesssim\kappa^{2}\|\tfrac{q^{\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\lesssim\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\kappa^{2}\int\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{r}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\,(\psi_{h}^{12})^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\,dx\right|\lesssim\kappa^{2}\|\tfrac{q}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}\lesssim\kappa^{-2}\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}.

For the first commutator term we estimate

\displaystyle\left|\int\Big(\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q^{\prime\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{r^{\prime\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\,\psi_{h}^{12}-\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}r)^{\prime\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\Big)\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\|[\psi_{h}^{6},\tfrac{\partial^{3}}{(2\varkappa-\partial)(4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2})}]q\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\|\tfrac{\partial^{3}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\Bigl\{\|[\psi_{h}^{6},\tfrac{\partial^{3}}{(2\varkappa-\partial)(4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2})}]q\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}+\|\tfrac{\partial^{3}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}(\psi_{h}^{6}q)\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\Bigr\}\|[\psi_{h}^{6},\tfrac{\partial^{3}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}+\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}.

The second commutator term is bounded similarly:

\displaystyle\left|\kappa^{2}\int\Big(\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{q^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{r^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\,\psi_{h}^{12}-\mathbf{R}[\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}},\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}r)^{\prime\prime}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}]\Big)\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}\|\tfrac{(\psi_{h}^{6}q)^{\prime\prime}}{\sqrt{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}}\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}+\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\varkappa}}^{2}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

We now turn to the proof of Lemma
5.3
. Here we will use the estimates of Lemmas
2.6
and
2.7
to obtain bounds for the tails of the series defining
g_{12},g_{21},\gamma
. However, these estimates are not sufficient to capture cancellations that occur for several quartic terms in the currents
j_{\mathrm{NLS}}^{\mathrm{diff}}
and
j_{\mathrm{mKdV}}^{\mathrm{diff}}
. For this reason, we start by proving several quadrilinear estimates that are designed to capture the additional smallness that arises from these cancellations.

For any
\kappa\geq 1
and multi-index
\beta\in\{0,1,2\}^{4}
we introduce the class
S(\beta;\kappa)
of smooth symbols
m\colon\mathbb{R}^{4}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
that may be written as

(5.23)

m(\xi;\kappa)=C\tfrac{\xi_{1}^{\beta_{1}}\xi_{2}^{\beta_{2}}\xi_{3}^{\beta_{3}}\xi_{4}^{\beta_{4}}}{(\kappa^{2}+\xi_{1}^{2})(\kappa^{2}+\xi_{2}^{2})(\kappa^{2}+\xi_{3}^{2})(\kappa^{2}+\xi_{4}^{2})},

for a constant
C\in\mathbb{C}
. We write
m[f_{1},\ldots,f_{4}]
for the paraproduct with this symbol.

While it is often natural to consider paraproducts as multilinear operators, we shall only be applying them to
q
and to objects subordinate to
q
, in the sense of (
5.24
). Thus, it is more natural to view these paraproducts as polynomial-like functions of
q
. When it comes to estimating these nonlinear expressions, the first step will always be to isolate the two highest frequency terms and use local smoothing to control them. (Integrability in time forbids using local smoothing for more than two factors.) Correspondingly, a multilinear point of view would lead to right-hand sides containing a sum over all permutations of the arguments. Here we see the virtue of phrasing them as nonlinear estimates and of subordinating their arguments to
q
.

For the NLS we have the following lemma:

Lemma 5.10

(Quartic estimate for the NLS)
.

Let
|\varkappa|\geq\kappa^{\frac{2}{3}}\geq 1
and the Schwartz functions
q,f\in\mathcal{C}([-1,1];B_{\delta}\cap\mathscr{S})
satisfy

(5.24)

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|f\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{NLS}_{\kappa}}\lesssim{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{NLS}_{\kappa}}.

Let
m\in S(\beta;\kappa)
where
1\leq|\beta|\leq 5
and at most one
\beta_{j}=2
. Then we have the paraproduct estimate

(5.25)

\bigl\|m[q,r,q,\psi_{h}^{12}\tfrac{f}{2\varkappa+\partial}]\bigr\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}\lesssim\kappa^{|\beta|-7-\frac{4}{3}(2s+1)}\delta^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{NLS}_{\kappa}}^{2},

where the implicit constant is independent of
\kappa
,
\varkappa
, and
h\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\psi
is as in (
1.5
).

Proof.

By space-translation invariance, we may assume
h=0
.

By Bernstein’s inequality, for
0\leq j\leq 2
we may bound

\displaystyle\tfrac{N^{j}}{(\kappa+N)^{2}}\|(\psi^{3}q)_{N}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{-2}N^{j-s-\frac{5}{2}}\min\{N^{\frac{5}{2}},\kappa N,\kappa^{2}\}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{NLS}_{\kappa}}

\displaystyle\tfrac{N^{j}}{(\kappa+N)^{2}}\|\big(\psi^{3}\tfrac{f}{2\varkappa+\partial}\big)_{N}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\varkappa|^{-(s+\frac{1}{2})}(|\varkappa|+N)^{s-\frac{1}{2}}\kappa^{-2}N^{j-s-\frac{5}{2}}\min\{N^{\frac{5}{2}},\kappa N,\kappa^{2}\}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|q\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\mathrm{NLS}_{\kappa}},

which we will use to estimate high frequency terms. Using Bernstein’s inequality again, we also find that for
0\leq j\leq 2
,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{M\leq N}\tfrac{M^{j}}{(\kappa+M)^{2}}\|(\psi^{3}q)_{M}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}\lesssim\kappa^{-2}N^{\frac{1}{2}-s}\min\{N^{j},\kappa^{j}\}\delta,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{M\leq N}\tfrac{M^{j}}{(\kappa+M)^{2}}\|\big(\psi^{3}\tfrac{f}{2\varkappa+\partial}\big)_{M}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}\!\lesssim|\varkappa|^{-(s+\frac{1}{2})}(|\varkappa|+N)^{s-\frac{1}{2}}\kappa^{-2}N^{\frac{1}{2}-s}\min\{N^{j},\kappa^{j}\}\delta,

which we will use to estimate low frequency terms.

For dyadic
N_{j}\geq 1
we write

\displaystyle m_{N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{4}}:=m\bigl[\psi^{-3}(\psi^{3}q)_{N_{1}},\psi^{-3}(\psi^{3}r)_{N_{2}},\psi^{-3}(\psi^{3}q)_{N_{3}},\psi^{9}(\psi^{3}\tfrac{f}{2\varkappa+\partial})_{N_{4}}\bigr],

so that

\bigl\|m[q,r,q,\psi^{12}\tfrac{f}{2\varkappa+\partial}]\bigr\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}\leq\sum\limits_{N_{j}\geq 1}\bigl\|m_{N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{4}}\bigr\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}.

As the estimates will be symmetric in the first three terms, we may assume that
N_{1}\geq N_{2}\geq N_{3}
. Our strategy will be to bound the two highest frequency terms in
L^{2}_{t,x}
to take advantage of the local smoothing norms, and the two lowest frequencies in
L^{\infty}_{t,x}
. Concretely, when
N_{4}\leq N_{2}
, we apply Lemma
2.8
, to obtain

\displaystyle\bigl\|m_{N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{4}}\bigr\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\psi^{3}\tfrac{\partial^{\beta_{1}}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}\psi^{-3}(\psi^{3}q)_{N_{1}}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\psi^{3}\tfrac{\partial^{\beta_{2}}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}\psi^{-3}(\psi^{3}q)_{N_{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\|\psi^{3}\tfrac{\partial^{\beta_{3}}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}\psi^{-3}(\psi^{3}q)_{N_{3}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}\|\psi^{-9}\tfrac{\partial^{\beta_{4}}}{4\kappa^{2}-\partial^{2}}\psi^{9}(\psi^{3}\tfrac{f}{2\varkappa+\partial})_{N_{4}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tfrac{N_{1}^{\beta_{1}}N_{2}^{\beta_{2}}N_{3}^{\beta_{3}}N_{4}^{\beta_{4}}}{(\kappa+N_{1})^{2}(\kappa+N_{2})^{2}(\kappa+N_{3})^{2}(\kappa+N_{4})^{2}}\|(\psi^{3}q)_{N_{1}}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|(\psi^{3}q)_{N_{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\|(\psi^{3}q)_{N_{3}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}\|(\psi^{3}\tfrac{f}{2\varkappa+\partial})_{N_{4}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}},

whereas, when
N_{4}>N_{2}
we obtain instead

\displaystyle\bigl\|m_{N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},N_{4}}\bigr\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}
